# DIVERGENCE.AI SITE CONTRACT v2

**Status:** CORRECTED STANDALONE DECISION + IMPLEMENTATION AUTHORITY  
**Supersedes:** `DIVERGENCE-AI-SITE-CONTRACT.md` and `DIVERGENCE-AI-FIX-ALL-IMPLEMENTATION-AUTHORITY.md` as claims of complete coverage.  
**Production protection:** `build` is not an implementation target for this repair contract.  
**Audited frozen baseline:** `frozen-implementation-v1@18e4345f703bfe62c8a5fca87b1b2a11a549be4c`.  
**Verified historical source checkpoint:** `cowork-complete-preview-20260823@16beca26c305bd9bdae088eb8e977ca1e9730747`. **Active governed work branch:** `horizontal-layer-completion-v1`; prior completion claims are not accepted as evidence of v2 compliance.

## 0. What changed from v1

The v1 Contract mapped its own 156-control inventory in detail, but it failed to prove that 156 was the complete decision universe. The upstream recovery found approved/recovered provider, connection, cost, large-job, evaluation, desktop and Developer requirements that were absent, under-atomic, or wrongly excluded.

v2 therefore keeps the useful 156 mappings and expands the permanent denominator by **62** IDs to **218**. It also expands workflows from **34** to **48**, repair groups from **26** to **39**, open decisions to **7**, and acceptance-test handles from the old **190** to **266**.

**Critical correction:** ordinary users still have one adaptive interface; that does not erase the separate operator-only Developer domain. Developer Mode is now documented instead of being thrown away under one `OUT_OF_SCOPE` row.

## 0.1 Completion rule

A coding or auditing AI may not call the product complete because it builds, because unit tests are green, because Cowork says all repairs are done, or because every old 156 ID was accounted for. Completion uses the **218-ID v2 authority denominator plus 48 workflows and the applicable evaluation gates**.


## 1. Canonical Decision Authority v2

## DIVERGENCE.AI — CANONICAL DECISION AUTHORITY v2

**Status:** CORRECTED AUTHORITY BASE — supersedes the old Site Contract as the denominator for completeness.

### 1. Authority rules

1. Explicit approved/user-set decisions control only the scope they actually decide.
2. Named frozen/canonical authorities control their stated scope.
3. An older approved decision remains valid until an authorized source explicitly supersedes that decision.
4. Source code, commits, tests, and deployments prove implementation/evidence; they do not create product requirements by existing.
5. An unresolved conflict remains a conflict. Timestamps do not choose the winner.
6. `OUT_OF_SCOPE` for one acceptance surface never means `OMIT FROM MASTER DECISION RECORD`.

### 2. Scoped Developer-Mode reconciliation

- **Ordinary users:** one adaptive interface with `Show advanced controls`; no ordinary user must identify as a developer.
- **Normal billing/provider use:** never requires Developer Mode and cannot rely on Developer approval.
- **Operator/project-owner domain:** Developer Mode is separately tracked below and must be reachable/functional for its intended operator.
- These statements do not contradict one another because they govern different scopes.

### 5. Old Contract disposition

- The old 156 Feature IDs are **not discarded**. Their detailed control/link/button mappings remain useful evidence and are carried forward.
- This authority adds **62** permanent requirement IDs that were missing, too coarse, or wrongly excluded.
- Corrected control/requirement universe: **156 existing Feature IDs + 62 new/expanded IDs = 218 permanent requirement/control IDs**.
- `SRC-DEV-01` is corrected from a blanket Developer exclusion to a cross-scope rule; `SPEC-DV-*` now owns the actual operator Developer domain.
- The old `FIX ALL` file is superseded because its repair universe was derived from the incomplete denominator.

### 6. Authority audit

- Recovered upstream decision/task rows: **59**.

- Developer Mode silently omitted: **NO**.
- Provider/cost/large-job decisions silently omitted: **NO**.

**VERDICT: PASS AS THE CORRECTED DECISION DENOMINATOR.**


## 2. Upstream Decision-Recovery Audit

## DIVERGENCE.AI — UPSTREAM DECISION RECOVERY AUDIT v1

**Purpose:** Repair the failed completeness premise of the prior Site Contract by auditing the decision universe *before* auditing the Contract.

**Prior Site Contract status:** **SUPERSEDED AS A CLAIM OF COMPLETE DECISION COVERAGE.** It remains useful as a source/code comparison for the items it actually contains, but it is not safe to call a complete implementation authority until the omissions below are incorporated.

### Authority rule used

1. Explicit approved/user-set decisions control only the scope they actually decide.
2. Named frozen/canonical authorities control their stated scope.
3. An older approved decision remains valid until something with authority explicitly supersedes it.
4. Code/commits prove implementation history, not product approval.
5. Unresolved conflicts stay unresolved; timestamps do not choose the winner.
6. OUT_OF_SCOPE for ordinary user-site acceptance does **not** mean OMIT FROM THE DECISION RECORD.

### Evidence base actually checked

- `DIVERGENCE-AI-BRANCH-DECISION-LEDGER.md` — read in full, 721 lines; GitHub archaeology across surviving branches/PRs/commits.
- `DIVERGENCE-AI-COMPREHENSIVE-SPECIFICATION.md` — approved GREEN decision traceability and supersession table.
- `DIVERGENCE-AI-COLORED-DECISION-MAP.docx` — GREEN route source.
- `01-FROZEN-SPEC-v1.1-PROVIDER-NEUTRAL-AMENDMENT.md` — approved as part of Packet v1.
- `05-COST-CREDITS-FREE-FIRST-SPEC.md` — approved cost/free-first packet.
- Approval conversation showing explicit user `approved` for Packet v1 on 2026-08-14.
- `PENDING-INTEGRATIONS.md`, `BUILD-LOG.md`, `CANON.md`, `DECISIONS_LOG.md`, `DECISIONS_INDEX.md` and relevant GitHub history as implementation/decision-history evidence.
- `Claude-Cowork setup-20260823-0424.md` for later Developer/async-testing requirements and implementation history.
- Existing `DIVERGENCE-AI-SITE-CONTRACT.md` for coverage comparison.

| DR-057 | Decision recovery prerequisite | The exhaustive GitHub branch decision audit was explicitly identified as a prerequisite before implementation; the decision map was not to be assumed comprehensive until that audit was complete. | RECOVERED PROCESS REQUIREMENT | 2026-08-14 conversation + Branch Decision Ledger | Documentation governance | MISSING_FROM_FINAL_AUDIT_LOGIC | Final Contract claimed universe completeness from its own inventory | Make upstream decision-universe coverage a mandatory gate before any 100% completeness claim. |
| DR-058 | Authority resolution | Approved decision-ledger entries outrank recovered canonical behavior; mature code fills only unsettled behavior; images govern visuals/placement; unapproved experiments do not become requirements. | RECOVERED AUTHORITY RULE | Branch Decision Ledger §23 | Documentation governance | PARTIAL | Contract had a hierarchy but later text incorrectly simplified it to latest correction wins | Replace any 'latest wins' shortcut with explicit-scope supersession + conflict handling. |
| DR-059 | No circular completeness | 156/156 control coverage does not prove all approved decisions were recovered; completeness denominator must come from audited authority sources, not from the generated Contract inventory itself. | CORRECTION REQUIRED BY THIS AUDIT | Failure exposed by Developer Mode and approval-packet omissions | Documentation governance | MISSING | old Sections 3/15/19 | Invalidate old global completeness claims and recalculate only after decision recovery. |

### Audit totals

- Recovered decision/task domains recorded: **59**
- Rows exposing a Contract coverage defect or required split: **34**
- `CORRECTLY_NOT_PROMOTED`: **1**
- `MISSING`: **17**
- `MISSING_FROM_FINAL_AUDIT_LOGIC`: **1**
- `MISSING_FROM_SCOPE_REGISTER`: **1**
- `NEEDS_ATOMIC_SPLIT`: **1**
- `PARTIAL`: **12**
- `PRESENT`: **24**
- `WRONGLY_EXCLUDED`: **2**

### Concrete proof the old completeness claim failed

- Existing Contract occurrences of `Fable`: **0**.
- Existing Contract occurrences of `BYOK`: **0**.
- Existing Contract occurrences of `browser companion`: **0**.
- Existing Contract occurrences of `receipt`: **0**.
- Existing Contract occurrences of `idempotency`: **0**.
- Existing Contract occurrences of `large-job`: **0**.
- Existing Contract occurrences of `Async Testing`: **0**.
- Existing Contract occurrences of `Draft Queue`: **0**.
- Existing Contract occurrences of `Preview Request`: **0**.
- Existing Contract occurrences of `Mark Sent`: **0**.

Those zero-count checks do not by themselves define authority. They demonstrate that approved/recovered concepts identified by the authority audit were not even represented in the purportedly complete Contract.

### Developer Mode correction

The prior Contract's single `SRC-DEV-01 = OUT_OF_SCOPE` row is not sufficient.

The corrected interpretation is:

- **Ordinary user site:** the approved GREEN design remains one adaptive interface with `Show advanced controls`; ordinary users do not need to choose a User/Developer mode.
- **Billing/provider behavior:** Developer Mode must never be required to use normal billing/provider controls and must never bypass production billing safeguards.
- **Developer/operator domain:** if retained for the project, its requirements must be documented independently even though it is excluded from ordinary user-site acceptance.
- **Async testing:** the later requested/implemented feature family must be tracked: parallel/recoverable drafts, preview/dry-run, recovery, rapid switching. Exact implementation details that were never explicitly approved remain implementation evidence, not automatic product law.
- **Heavy-use personal Developer goal:** this is a recorded user requirement that still needs a final approved design; omission is not an acceptable resolution.

### What this audit invalidates

The following prior claims are withdrawn:

- `156/156` as proof that every approved product decision was recovered.
- `190/190` as proof that every required feature/workflow had an acceptance test.
- the statement that the Site Contract was globally complete/safe as a standalone implementation authority.
- any `FIX ALL` instruction whose universe is limited to the old 26 Repair Queue groups.

Those numbers remain arithmetic facts about the old Contract's own inventory, nothing more.

### Required next production step

Rebuild the canonical Contract denominator from this recovered authority set plus the full approved packet, then regenerate Feature IDs, conflicts, repairs, acceptance tests, evidence, and traceability. The old Contract must be treated as an input to that rebuild, not as the authority that defines what exists.

### Audit verdict

**FAIL — THE PRIOR SITE CONTRACT IS NOT SAFE AS A COMPLETE DECISION/IMPLEMENTATION AUTHORITY.**

**PASS — this upstream audit successfully establishes concrete missing/incorrectly-scoped decision families that the rebuild must include.**

It was not attached to a commit or ref. No tracked repository file, branch head, commit or deployment was changed. The incident remains disclosed for final hardening/handoff because the Contract production process is intended to be read-only.

## 6. Backend / API Map of Audited Frozen Baseline

## SECTION 5 — Implementation / Backend / API Map

**Primary branch:** `frozen-implementation-v1`  
**Audited commit:** `18e4345f703bfe62c8a5fca87b1b2a11a549be4c`

### 5.1 Backend reality in one paragraph

The frozen web build is mostly a browser-local React/Zustand application with IndexedDB persistence. Server work is concentrated in seven Edge/serverless routes. The main `/api/proxy` route is Anthropic-specific; four additional provider proxies are used as Multi-AI debate partners; `/api/fetch-url` retrieves URL context; `/api/verify-access` checks the shared app-access password state. There is no server-side user account/allowance ledger in the audited branch.

### 5.2 Endpoint registry

| ID | Endpoint | Method | Frontend caller/use | Request shape | Response shape | Auth | Provider/upstream | Cost/credit | Privacy/data boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| API-01 | `/api/proxy` | POST | `proxyClient.ts`; managed pipeline; paid State Detection/Multi-AI Claude calls | JSON `{model, system?, messages[], stream?, maxTokens?, extendedThinking?}` | Anthropic Messages API response passed through; browser client parses JSON or SSE; token usage read from Anthropic usage fields | Requires server `APP_ACCESS_PASSWORD` to be configured **and** a matching `x-app-password` header; server `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is separate | Anthropic Messages API | Client authorizes/deducts local credits before selected calls; server route itself has no user-credit ledger | Raw prompt/messages leave device for Anthropic; server API key remains server-side |
| API-02 | `/api/proxy-openai` | POST | `services/debate/client.ts` | `{model, system, input, maxTokens?}` | Normalized `{text}` or `{error}` | Configured `APP_ACCESS_PASSWORD` + matching `x-app-password`; server OpenAI key | OpenAI partner adapter | No server credit enforcement; calling Multi-AI code performs client-side estimated authorization first | Question/system prompt sent to OpenAI |
| API-03 | `/api/proxy-google` | POST | `services/debate/client.ts` | `{model, system, input, maxTokens?}` | Normalized `{text}` or `{error}` | Configured `APP_ACCESS_PASSWORD` + matching `x-app-password`; separate server Google key | Google partner adapter | No server credit ledger | Question/system prompt sent to Google |
| API-04 | `/api/proxy-xai` | POST | `services/debate/client.ts` | `{model, system, input, maxTokens?}` | Normalized `{text}` or `{error}` | Configured `APP_ACCESS_PASSWORD` + matching `x-app-password`; separate server xAI key | xAI partner adapter | No server credit ledger | Question/system prompt sent to xAI |
| API-05 | `/api/proxy-deepseek` | POST | `services/debate/client.ts` | `{model, system, input, maxTokens?}` | Normalized `{text}` or `{error}` | Configured `APP_ACCESS_PASSWORD` + matching `x-app-password`; separate server DeepSeek key | DeepSeek partner adapter | No server credit ledger | Question/system prompt sent to DeepSeek |
| API-06 | `/api/fetch-url` | POST | `services/context/urlContext.ts` | `{url}` | `{contentType, content}` on success; error JSON on failure | Configured `APP_ACCESS_PASSWORD` + matching `x-app-password`; no AI-provider key | HTTP GET to the user-supplied target through `urlFetchHandler` | No Divergence AI-model credit charge | The requested URL leaves the browser; fetched page content returns to browser and is persisted if attached |
| API-07 | `/api/verify-access` | Access-check request | Intended access-gate bootstrap | Header may contain `x-app-password`; no provider payload | JSON `{requiresPassword, ok}` | No prior password required to call endpoint | None | No charge | Only access-check state; does not call an AI provider |

### 5.2A Endpoint failure behavior

| Endpoint class | Source-level failure behavior |
|---|---|
| `/api/proxy` | Access failure returns 401; wrapper applies an in-memory per-IP rate limit and can return 429; handler returns 405 for wrong method, 500 for missing Anthropic key, 400 for invalid JSON/model/messages, and 502 for upstream network failure; provider HTTP status/body is otherwise passed through. |
| Partner proxies | Access failure returns 401; shared partner handler validates POST/body/model/input, requires the provider key, applies a 45-second timeout, preserves upstream HTTP failure status without echoing provider response content, and normalizes unusable/non-JSON replies to 502. |
| `/api/fetch-url` | Access failure returns 401; URL-fetch handler validates/fetches the supplied URL and returns structured error JSON on rejection/network/content failure. |
| `/api/verify-access` | Does not call a provider and is intentionally callable before a password is known; reports access state only. |

### 5.3 Feature/workflow implementation chains

| Chain ID | Feature/workflow | Frontend → implementation chain | API/backend | Storage | Failure handling | Cost/credit | Retention/privacy |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IMP-01 | Universal/local prepared-request handoff | Composer → CenterColumn.handleSubmit → detectStateLocally → compileMeaningPacket → buildAiReadyRequest → StateReviewPanel/ReviewReadyRequest → handoff message → ImportResponseDialog | No API route for prompt compilation; user may manually open/copy to destination | Local session/account state; conversation persisted by autosave | Local validation; review/cancel; import confirmation | No Divergence model credits on local wrapper/manual handoff | Raw message/context stays local until user deliberately hands it to an external destination. **Defect:** MeaningPacket keeps context labels rather than content. |
| IMP-02 | Managed Anthropic answer/translation path | Composer → CenterColumn.handleSubmit → estimate → authorizeEstimatedCost → startRun → observePipeline/runPipeline → proxyClient | `POST /api/proxy` | Session conversation + local credit ledger/telemetry | Authorization cancellation/insufficient credits; proxy non-2xx throws; pipeline resilience handles stage failures | Estimated credits are confirmed/deducted client-side before the call; no server user ledger | Prepared/raw content leaves device for Anthropic. Current selected Destination AI is not consistently honored. |
| IMP-03 | Paid State Detection | CenterColumn.startRun → detectState → proxyClient.complete | `POST /api/proxy` | State pills/corrections persisted locally | Detection outcome is non-throwing at service boundary; abort on resubmit | Token usage tracked; route is a paid provider call when used | Raw input sent to Anthropic for classification |
| IMP-04 | File context | AttachContextControls → uploadFiles/file validation/OCR → addContextItem | No web API for ordinary file parsing in the shown chain | ContextItem stored in session persistence | Rejected-file list; OCR/read failure surfaced; draft preserved | No provider credit implied by attachment itself | Selected file content is retained locally in session state; later provider request may transmit included content if implementation actually uses it |
| IMP-05 | URL context | AttachContextControls → fetchUrlContext | `POST /api/fetch-url` | ContextItem returned to session persistence | Bad URL, network failure, proxy error, empty extraction, per-item/session-size rejection return user-facing typed errors | No AI-model credit | URL sent to Divergence fetch endpoint and target website; extracted content stored locally. **Access defect:** browser gate does not establish required password. |
| IMP-06 | Variables | AttachContextControls / Customize → session/account variable setters → Composer substituteVariables | No API | Session variables or account-level browser persistence | Name validation; substitution happens before request construction | No direct charge | Variable values remain local until substituted text is later sent/handed off |
| IMP-07 | Quick Actions/session lifecycle | QuickActionsRow → buildSessionRecord → account/session store actions | No API | Browser IndexedDB or desktop state database | Undo timer, confirmation dialogs; several semantics conflict with approved Trash/Save behavior | No direct charge | Session data remains local |
| IMP-08 | Sessions screen import/export | ScreenRouter SessionsScreen → FileReader/JSON parser; browser Blob/download for export | No API | Imported SessionRecord added to account store; export saved to user file | JSON shape checks are limited; file errors handled in UI path | No direct charge | Data stays local except where user saves/loads a file |
| IMP-09 | Multi-AI Debate | MultiAiActions → authorizeEstimatedCost → runDebate → Claude proxy + partner client | `/api/proxy` plus one or more of `/api/proxy-openai\|google\|xai\|deepseek` | Result held in component state; usage log stored locally | Per-participant failure yields partial/failed outcome; partner proxy timeout = 45s; retry per side | Client estimated authorization before calls; partner endpoints have no server credit ledger | Question may be sent to multiple external providers |
| IMP-10 | Multi-AI Consensus/Synthesis | MultiAiActions → authorizeEstimatedCost → runConsensus/runSynthesis → main proxy | `POST /api/proxy` | Component state + telemetry/usage local | Result status produces UI error/retry | Client estimated authorization/deduction | Debate transcript sent to Anthropic |
| IMP-11 | Cost authorization | Feature caller → getEstimatedCost… → authorizeEstimatedCost → CostConfirm → accountStore.deductCredits → saveNow | No server billing endpoint | Credit balance/ledger stored in account store persistence | Invalid estimate, cancel, insufficient credit; fallback to `window.confirm` if host absent | Estimated amount is deducted locally before provider call; developer mode bypasses deduction; no server entitlement/allowance authority | Financial state is browser-local. **Defect:** `paidFallbackEnabled` and `maxRequestCost` are not consumed by the primary execution decision. |
| IMP-12 | Browser persistence/recovery | main.tsx/loadPersistedState → Zustand hydrate; startAutosave/saveNow | No API | IndexedDB DB `divergence-ai`, object store `state`, keys `session` and `account`; 5-second autosave; pagehide/hidden flush | IndexedDB transaction is atomic; storage errors can still affect recovery | No charge | Conversation/context/settings remain on device; browser-local scope means no cross-device account truth |
| IMP-13 | Desktop persistence | persistence.ts → desktopBridge.state.save/load | Electron IPC to desktop process; no web API | Desktop database via bridge; one-time migration from browser IndexedDB | Bridge errors propagate to caller; desktop tests exist | No provider charge for persistence | Local desktop data remains on machine |
| IMP-14 | App access/password | Intended AppAccessGate → verify access → appAccessClient localStorage → API headers | `/api/verify-access`; all protected `/api/*` routes check `x-app-password` | Password client helper uses localStorage key `divergence-app-access-password` | Protected routes fail closed if server password is missing, client header is missing, or values differ | No charge to verify access | Shared password can reside in browser localStorage. **Defect:** frozen AppAccessGate is currently a passthrough, so normal user flow never asks/stores it. |
| IMP-15 | Settings provider-neutral controls | ProviderNeutralSettings / AdvancedControls → session/account setters | No API for connection setup | Mostly session/account browser persistence | UI-level input constraints only | Paid fallback/max fields are display/state only unless consumed by execution; currently not | No real provider-account/OAuth connection data exists in audited UI |
| IMP-16 | Theme/visibility/layout | VisibilityMenu / AppearanceSettings → account store → theme/layout effects | No API | Account browser persistence | Immediate state mutation/reset | No charge | Local preference data only. Current `Original` layout option conflicts with frozen visual authority. |
| IMP-17 | Web account/plans/payments | SubscriptionUI/Settings → accountStore manual request/plan fields | No real remote account/payment endpoint identified | Local account-store records only | Manual/local approval scaffolding | Not a real payment/entitlement system | No server user identity; cross-device data/allowance cannot be authoritative |

### 5.4 Auth and secret boundaries

- Provider API keys are read from server environment variables in Edge wrappers; they are not embedded in the browser bundle.
- Protected API routes call `isAuthorized()`, which returns false when `APP_ACCESS_PASSWORD` is missing **or** when the request lacks/mismatches `x-app-password`. In other words, the protected routes require both a configured server password and the matching client header.
- `/api/verify-access` is deliberately callable before authentication and returns only `{requiresPassword, ok}`. **Source mismatch:** when `APP_ACCESS_PASSWORD` is absent, `checkAccess()` reports `{requiresPassword:false, ok:true}`, while protected routes still reject because `isAuthorized()` returns false without a configured password.
- Browser helper `appAccessClient.ts` stores the shared app password in localStorage if something calls its setter.
- **Frozen defect:** `AppAccessGate.tsx` is currently a pass-through and never invokes that password setup flow.
- Desktop provider access uses `desktopBridge`/IPC rather than exposing server keys in React code.

### 5.5 Storage and retention map

| Data class | Storage | Contents | Retention behavior |
|---|---|---|---|
| Browser session/account state | IndexedDB `divergence-ai` → object store `state` → keys `session`, `account` | Draft, destination/model, translator, review, directness, techniques, context, conversation, state pills, variables, screen/methodology; account settings/content/credit ledger | Autosave every 5 seconds + pagehide/visibility flush |
| Browser app-access password | localStorage `divergence-app-access-password` | Shared app access password | Persists until explicitly cleared |
| Desktop state | Desktop bridge/database | Session + account snapshots | Desktop save/load; browser IndexedDB migration path |
| Server AI credentials | Server environment | Anthropic and partner-provider API keys; APP_ACCESS_PASSWORD | Not written to user-state stores |
| Telemetry/cost history | Browser memory/account persistence depending service/store | Usage estimates, local credit ledger, some telemetry | Not a server source of truth |

A developer can trace each backend-dependent feature from the React caller to service code, endpoint, authorization boundary, upstream provider, local storage, failure path, cost implication, and privacy boundary.

### 7.2 Canvas and scaling

- **Logical product canvas:** `1600 × 1024` from the comprehensive/provider-neutral specification and current `AppShell` data authority.
- **Reference capture/canonical visual viewport recorded by `FROZEN-LAYOUT.md`:** `1543 × 1019`. This is treated as the visual reference capture, not a replacement for the 1600×1024 logical coordinate system.
- **Current code scaling:** `AppShell` computes `min(0.72, widthScale, heightScale)` with a 48-pixel edge gutter and floor `0.1`. Therefore the frozen canvas is capped at **72% scale** rather than freely expanding.
- **Document scrolling:** `html`, `body`, and `#root` use `overflow:hidden` in `frozen-reference.css`; the intended model is fixed outer canvas with bounded internal overflow.

### 7.5 Light-mode material contract
- Muted gray-beige marble, not bright white.
- Fine restrained gold veining; marble remains visible beneath all three regions.
- Pale translucent gray frosted glass for header, navigation, workspace, right rail, cards and controls.
- Thin white/silver/pale-gray borders; soft inset highlights and restrained exterior shadows.
- Black/deep-charcoal primary text and outline icons.
- Gold reserved for branding/structural accents.
- Electric blue reserved for active selections, connected paths/nodes and important data.
- Inactive navigation rows remain primarily transparent; active Talk to AI/Translate receives pale-blue active treatment.
- One coherent material system; no opaque disconnected sidebars/pills.

Current implementation uses `gold-marble-light-slab.png` plus a soft gray overlay and frosted panel tokens in `frozen-reference.css`.

### 7.6 Dark-mode contract
- Same frozen shell and geometry as light mode; theme changes material/color values, not component coordinates.
- Dark authority is `MAIN-Dark-LAYOUT-DIVERGENCE-FROZEN.svg`.
- Current CSS shifts gold/cyan/text/panel/border/shadow tokens and uses `gold-marble-dark-slab.png`.
- Dark mode must not create a different navigation architecture, different rail positions or a different interaction hierarchy.

### 7.7 Permanent visual prohibitions carried forward
- Alternate/original layout restoration or layout switch that reinterprets the frozen shell.
- Opaque dark sidebars in light mode.
- Separate solid pills behind inactive navigation rows.
- Unframed raw content directly over exposed marble.
- Unstyled native controls/scrollbars dividing major regions.
- Broken/duplicated branding.
- Detached credit/session-cost boxes or in-composition window controls.
- Random multicolor Quick Tools icons.
- Missing connector network.
- Background-only theme swapping that leaves the wrong material system.
- Approximate/inspired-by replacements for the reference.

#### Current deterministic visual-source conflict

`SRC-VIS-01`: the frozen current `VisibilityMenu` exposes `Original` and `Gold` layout choices even though `FROZEN-LAYOUT.md` permanently prohibits an `original`/alternate layout switch. **Source-level visual verdict: WRONG.**

### 7.8 Responsive/mobile scope

- **Approved desktop model:** fixed logical canvas that scales as a unit; outer document does not reflow into a conventional responsive/mobile layout.
- **Current code:** `body { min-width:1180px }` plus whole-canvas transform scaling. There is no mobile column stacking/reflow implementation in the inspected visual authority.
- **Mobile product support:** `UNDECIDED` as a separate product scope; the current implementation is effectively **desktop/fixed-canvas only**, with scaling rather than mobile redesign.
- A later mobile design cannot be inferred by an implementer; it requires an explicit approved truth source.

| V2-DQ-002 | Whether `Ctrl+S` is an approved Divergence shortcut for Sessions. | KB-04 | No approved source defines it. The current code alone cannot decide product behavior, and `Ctrl+S` normally invokes browser/page Save behavior. | Should Divergence.AI override `Ctrl+S` to open Sessions, or should that shortcut be removed? | UNDECIDED — current code is evidence only; do not treat the shortcut as approved until the user decides. | Determines keyboard navigation behavior and whether the app intentionally replaces the browser's normal Save shortcut. |
| V2-DQ-003 | Whether `Ctrl+L` is an approved Divergence shortcut for Templates. | KB-05 | No approved source defines it. The current code alone cannot create authority, and `Ctrl+L` normally focuses the browser address bar. | Should Divergence.AI override `Ctrl+L` to open Templates, or should that shortcut be removed? | UNDECIDED — current code is evidence only; do not treat the shortcut as approved until the user decides. | Determines whether keyboard navigation intentionally overrides a high-value browser navigation command. |

The current code alone cannot create authority, and `Ctrl+P` normally opens browser printing. | Should Divergence.AI override `Ctrl+P` to open Saved Prompts, or should that shortcut be removed? | UNDECIDED — current code is evidence only; do not treat the shortcut as approved until the user decides. | Determines whether the app intentionally replaces browser Print and whether Saved Prompts needs this shortcut in acceptance tests. |
| V2-DQ-005 | Whether Divergence.AI v1 supports a dedicated mobile/reflow layout or remains desktop/fixed-canvas only. | SRC-VIS-01; SPEC-SE-02 | Section 7 explicitly classifies mobile product support as UNDECIDED. No approved mobile truth source exists, and the current fixed-canvas desktop implementation cannot create a new mobile product requirement. | Should Divergence.AI v1 support a dedicated mobile/reflow layout, or should v1 remain desktop/fixed-canvas only with mobile support deferred? | UNDECIDED — do not invent mobile reflow; until decided, mobile is not an acceptance target and the approved desktop/fixed-canvas shell remains the only defined visual scope. | Changes responsive scope, visual acceptance tests, viewport support claims, and whether a separate mobile truth source must be designed and approved. |

Cowork proposed caching, UI rate limiting, free-tier-first routing and hard caps, but proposal/implementation is not the same as approval. | Which exact Developer Mode heavy-use routing/cost strategy is approved as the permanent design? | UNDECIDED — keep the goal and existing experimental mechanisms tracked; do not let this block unrelated repairs. | Determines the final Developer personal-use execution policy and its acceptance tests. |

### Coverage

- Old carried-forward repair groups: **26**.
- New decision-recovery repair groups: **13**.
- Total v2 repair groups: **39**.
- Deterministic new/expanded defects mapped: **60 / 60**.
- `SPEC-DV-09` remains UNDECIDED and is deliberately not implementation-forced.
- `SPEC-DS-01` is platform-scoped rather than used to block the web preview.

**VERDICT: PASS — every deterministic v2 addition is mapped to a repair group.**

## 12. Frozen-Baseline Evidence Index

## SECTION 14 — Evidence Index

**Primary implementation branch:** `frozen-implementation-v1`  
**Audited commit:** `18e4345f703bfe62c8a5fca87b1b2a11a549be4c`  
**Evidence date:** August 22, 2026

### 14.1 Evidence rules

- Source evidence proves what exists in the audited commit; it does **not** by itself prove matching-deployment end-to-end behavior.
- Live/browser evidence is separately identified. The current matching-deployment traversal blocker is evidence, not permission to infer `WORKING`.
- No secrets, tokens, API keys, or password values are included. Environment-variable and header **names** may be documented because they describe the security boundary without exposing a credential.
- Where the locked source comparison gives a file/component/function rather than an exact line range, this index preserves that precision and does not invent line numbers.

### Status accounting

- Existing 156 control statuses after Developer-scope correction: **{'MISSING': 21, 'WRONG': 65, 'PARTIAL': 46, 'UNTESTED': 16, 'UNDECIDED': 4, 'BROKEN': 4}**.
- New 62 requirement statuses: **{'WRONG': 5, 'MISSING': 19, 'PARTIAL': 34, 'OUT_OF_SCOPE': 1, 'BROKEN': 2, 'UNDECIDED': 1}**.
- Deterministic existing defects (`WRONG/PARTIAL/MISSING/BROKEN`): **136**.
- Deterministic new/expanded defects: **60**.
- Total deterministic defect IDs in the v2 denominator: **196**.

### Non-circularity proof

- The 218 denominator is not inferred from this matrix. It comes from 156 previously static-inventoried controls plus 62 permanent IDs created from the independent 59-row upstream authority recovery.
- The 59-row upstream recovery was built from designated decision/approval/branch-history sources before this matrix was calculated.
- `UNDECIDED` and platform-scoped items are counted as decisions/scope, not converted into fake implementation defects.
- No `WORKING` whole-product claim is made from source/test arithmetic.

### Cross-artifact integrity

- Every `SPEC-*` v2 addition appears in Canonical Decision Authority v2, Control & Behavior Map v2, and the v2 acceptance additions.
- Every deterministic v2 addition is covered by at least one `V2-RQ-*` repair.
- Decision-dependent Developer design is isolated in `V2-DQ-006`/`V2-DQ-007`; it does not block unrelated repairs.
- Cowork's unrelated `RQ-*` numbering is not used as authority.

**VERDICT: PASS — v2 traceability uses an independently recovered authority denominator rather than the old circular 156-ID universe.**


## 14. v2 Correction Manifest

## DIVERGENCE.AI — CONTRACT v2 CORRECTION MANIFEST

**Old Contract:** `DIVERGENCE-AI-SITE-CONTRACT.md` — superseded as a complete authority.
**Corrected denominator:** `DIVERGENCE-AI-CANONICAL-DECISION-AUTHORITY-v2.md`.

### 1. Why v1 failed

The v1 audit proved one-to-one coverage of its own inventory, but did not independently prove that the inventory contained every approved decision. The upstream decision audit found approved/recovered domains absent from the denominator.

### 2. Required structural corrections

- Retain the useful 156 atomic control rows.
- Add **62** permanent IDs for omitted/under-atomic provider, connection, cost, large-job, evaluation, learning, desktop and Developer requirements.
- Replace blanket `SRC-DEV-01 = OUT_OF_SCOPE` with scoped ordinary-user vs operator-domain treatment.
- Remove Local AI/Ollama from the normal target behavior under the later explicit user correction.
- Add Fable-under-Claude, BYOK, licensed OAuth, optional browser companion, provider adapters and refreshable registry as explicit requirements.
- Add auto-top-up, three-level caps, server reservation/reconciliation, receipts, nonnegative concurrent balance and idempotency as explicit cost requirements.
- Add resumable large-job processing and its checkpoint/evidence/quality/cost invariants.
- Add explicit evaluation-gate requirements.
- Add the missing Developer async-testing and heavy-use goal family.
- Replace any global `156/156`, `190/190`, or `all 26 repairs` completeness claim with v2-denominator totals.

## 15. Implementation semantics

- The approved behavior in this Contract is the target.
- The frozen-code behavior is evidence about the audited baseline, not permission to preserve a contradiction.
- Cowork preview evidence is a later implementation candidate and must be rechecked against v2 before it is credited.
- `BLOCKED`/`UNTESTED` live evidence never means `do not implement`; it means `do not claim verified live`.
- `UNDECIDED` blocks only its exact decision-dependent behavior.
- Do not modify `build`.
- Do not use Cowork's old `RQ-*` meanings as this Contract's repair identifiers.

## 16. Retired standalone FIX ALL command

The standalone command formerly stored here is superseded. Repair IDs remain coverage obligations, but they are not the work sequence. Any future `FIX ALL` instruction means: execute the next declared coherent batch of the current horizontal layer after the governed preflight and exact permission gates pass.

```text
Open the governed continuation repository in read-only mode. Read START-HERE-DIVERGENCE.md and run the structural preflight. If and only if the preflight passes, PERMISSIONS.yml authorizes the exact action, and CURRENT-LAYER-STATUS.md declares one coherent batch, execute that batch under HORIZONTAL-LAYER-COMPLETION-SYSTEM.md. Do not process repair groups sequentially and do not begin another layer without its gate and user authorization.
```
